The Reds lost their way after the teenager’s withdrawal in Sunday’s defeat to Arsenal as the midfielder shone on his first ever Premier League start

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Liverpool head coach Andoni Iraola grabbed Trey Nyoni and threw him in at the deep end. Sink or swim was the aim of the game. And the teenager kept his head above water, and then some.

The teenager made his first ever Premier League start on Sunday afternoon. The call was well deserved following a fine showing against Chelsea in the EFL Cup.

That it came against defending champions Arsenal was a huge show of faith in the midfielder. The Reds may have fallen to a narrow 1-0 home defeat to the Gunners, but Nyoni’s 59-minute display proved he’s ready for regular minutes in this Liverpool side.

You’d be forgiven for overlooking Nyoni’s showing at Anfield. Declan Rice, after all, was the star performer on Merseyside. Mikel Arteta’s ‘Master-of-all-Trades’ midfield man put in a dominant display in the middle of the park.

The assist for Kai Havertz’s one and only goal was the cherry on the icing atop the Arsenal cake.

Yet despite coming up against, arguably, one of the best midfielders in world football, Nyoni wasn’t overawed. Rather, he rose to the occasion to deliver a performance that perhaps convinced Iraola he is worthy of a starting spot at the expense of either Alexis Mac Allister or Ryan Gravenberch.

“I just tried to go out there and do what I do, tried to get on the ball as much as possible and bring the team forwards,” Nyoni told the official Liverpool website about making his debut in such a blockbuster event. Many would have been overwhelmed by the spotlight that came from such a moment. Nyoni, though, was buoyed on by the welcome of Arsenal.

The best features, fun and footballing quizzes, straight to your inbox every week.

The 19-year-old ended the game with the best pass success rate (96.4%) of all starters in Sunday’s match. Only teammates Virgil van Dijk (67) and Mac Allister (61) made more passes than the academy product (56). The former pair played the full 90, whereas the latter was withdrawn on the hour mark.

Nyoni was dictating the tempo in the middle of the park, and driving Liverpool forward. Only Arsenal goalkeeper David Raya (46) made more forward passes than the youngster (24) at the weekend. Fourteen line-breaking passes was the most during his time on the pitch.

Iraola’s decision to withdraw Nyoni in place of the returning Dominik Szoboszlai meant that, for a 15-minute spell, Liverpool ceded momentum as they struggled to gain a foothold on the game.

From the 76th-minute onwards, they again dominated possession, even if it amounted to nothing, but Nyoni’s substitution saw the tie swing back in Arsenal’s favour at a crucial time in the clash. It’s within this 15-minute window where Arteta’s men begin to turn the screw in games. Only Manchester United (33) have had more shots than Arsenal (26) between minutes 60 and 75. Twenty-four chances created ranks top.

Liverpool needed a player who was comfortable in possession to ensure Arsenal were unable to build upon Havertz’s early strike. Of course, the momentum swing didn’t amount to another Gunners goal. Conversely, Liverpool would have enjoyed a more comfortable period of play had Nyoni remained on the pitch.

He brings some much-needed composure in a key area, a quality that had been found wanting following the departure of Curtis Jones. The sidelined Ryan Gravenberch brings the drive from deep, while Mac Allister offers the nitty-gritty, off the ball work to help protect the defence. Nyoni, meanwhile, delivers the forward thrust with his vision and distribution, qualities that far exceed both Gravenberch and Mac Allister, particularly in a 4-2-3-1 setup.

And having been dispossessed just four times in all competitions this season, Nyoni’s press resistance is another important trait. He’s comfortable receiving the ball from the centre-backs, before turning and setting his side on the frontfoot. Even under pressure, you’d back the up-and-coming Reds star to wriggle his way out of danger.

At his age, Nyoni, physically, still has some growing to do. Iraola can be expected to manage his minutes accordingly in order to avoid injury stunting his growth. Regardless, it’s clear that Nyoni is ready for a more prominent role in this Liverpool side.

Former boss Arne Slot used Nyoni sparingly, despite speaking highly of the player. In a difficult second, and final, season in the Anfield dugout, the Dutchman opted for experience. Injuries have perhaps forced Iraola’s hand this time around, but Nyoni has grabbed the opportunity to consolidate a starting spot with both hands.

November 19, 2026 is a date that’s been etched into my memory, circled in red on my calendar and set up as a day where I’m considering using my PTO to enjoy it.

Rockstar Games’ Grand Theft Auto VI has made plenty of headlines (some good, some bad). There’s plenty to be excited about from a gameplay perspective, such as its dynamic weather, deep relationship systems and vastly improved behaviors for NPCs (Non-Playable Characters). Not being able to truly celebrate a midnight launch for the game since there’s no actual physical disc for the game is a major bummer, though.

Even with all the controversy surrounding GTA 6, I’m still hyped beyond belief for what is undoubtedly the biggest game release of the year. As soon as November came calling, I tapped into ChatGPT and requested its help with preparing for its impending launch.

ChatGPT gave me a list of tasks to do, games worth checking out and gaming gear I should consider picking up to further enhance my GTA 6 experience.

When I presented this prompt (“Grand Theft Auto VI is releasing on November 19. What are the best ways to prepare for its launch?”, ChatGPT gave me a bunch of activities to tend to before the big day arrives. A lot of them stood out as objectives I’ve already considered doing, such as muting any GTA 6-related keywords on social media to avoid spoilers.

The one pre-launch ritual it told me to pay close attention to is to get my console storage ready for the game itself. Rockstar already announced that their latest open-world experience will take up 212 GB on PS5 consoles and pre-loading for that device will start on Tuesday, November 10 at midnight local time (Eastern Time Zone, for me). My PS5 Pro has 685 GB of free storage, so my console is more than ready for the pre-load process.

Since I want to save my PS5 Pro’s internal storage for GTA 6 and enjoy games similar to it on my Xbox Series X, I felt it was time to sift through a bunch of titles that deliver a mix of open-world crime gameplay, Florida-inspired adventures, immersive driving and incredible storytelling. After I asked ChatGPT a follow-up question (“And what gams should I play to get myself in the right mood for Grand Theft Auto VI?”, ChatGPT curated 10 picks I should consider trying out.

Whether you like your coffee black or with milk, the Black Friday sales are the best moment of the year to finally upgrade your home coffee making setup. The deals have begun already, and I've spotted a trio of great offers, including a massive £250 off the Sage The Barista Touch at Currys.

This is a Bean to Cup coffee machine, which means it grinds your beans as well as making the coffee. Fresh grounds are one of the keys to getting the perfect brew, and an all-in-one machine saves having to buy separate appliances and find the space and sockets for them.

See below for more details on this and other early Black Friday coffee machine deals available now.

The Barista Touch is a complete home coffee-making hub that maintains the look of a classic espresso machine. It has an integrated precision conical burr grinder, a ThermoJet espresso system and an auto steam wand optimised for latte art. The touchscreen makes it quick and easy to adjust water temperature, coffee strength and milk texture, and you can save up to eight personalised programs.

Traditionalists might turn their nose up at the Ninja AutoBarista as it foregoes the classic portafilter for a hands-off, push-button mechanism, but that does make make life easy on a busy morning. It has a steel conical grinder with a built-in digital scale to weigh your coffee and an automated Grind IQ feature that attempts to auto-calibrate settings based on flow and pressure. Beans go into the hopper, and coffee drops straight into the brew group inside the machine.

If you want a simple morning espresso without the distraction of digital displays and an infinite array of possibilities, the much simpler De'Longhi Dedica Style offers great value and a sleek, classic design. There's no grinder or programming, but it's a reliable, beautifully designed machine that can make two espressos at once and has a wand to froth or steam milk for cappuccinos, macchiatos or lattes.

A New UK Porn Ban Sparks Fears of Unprecedented Sex Censorship

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A new UK law banning porn featuring choking and “fauxcest,” or simulated incest, may have unintended consequences for content creators and smut fans all over the world, some experts warn.

As of November 6, amendments to the Crime and Policing Act will outlaw the “possession or publication of pornographic images of strangulation or suffocation”—a law intended to curb violence against women and girls. Yet some experts warn that the legislation is written broadly enough that it could potentially criminalize far more innocuous content, including “breast smothering,” “jeans facesitting,” and even potentially oral sex, ostensibly because people could have their airways impeded during these activities.

“I would encourage both content creators and consumers to underscore the risks of this law,” says Myles Jackman, an obscenity lawyer in the UK. “Because to me, it genuinely seems like one of the greatest acts of suppression of free speech in global history.”

The new amendments were inspired by a lengthy pornography review authored by Baroness Gabby Bertin, a Conservative member of the UK House of Lords. The legislation bans various depictions of sex acts, including “pornographic images of strangulation or suffocation,” as well as content showing performers role-playing as minors under the age of 16.

It also takes aim at “fauxcest,” an extremely popular porn niche that depicts simulated sexual relations between family members who are not related by blood, such as stepsiblings or stepparents. In Bertin’s review recommending the ban on such content, she cites “anecdotal evidence” suggesting that fauxcest “can be used between an abuser and child to permit interfamily child sex abuse,” describing it as a “fig-leaf” of sorts for actual sexual abuse.

Under the new law, those caught distributing or in possession of such content face up to five years in prison and a fine.

The impending UK legislation has prompted panic among some adult creators, many of whom are currently combing through their content libraries and removing clips that could get swept up in the ban.

“The problem is always how broad [the legislation] is,” says Alexandra Snow, an adult content creator and educator. “And we won't know how they're going to interpret it until they start making people's lives hell.”

Snow says she is particularly concerned about how the aspect of the legislation pertaining to “suffocation or strangulation” will affect kink creators, who may make content related to collaring or leather fetishes. She’s also worried about AI image-recognition software, which is already in widespread use among the platforms, potentially flagging content that isn’t violative at all. “They use a very broad, heavy-handed approach,” she says.

Cami Strella, an OnlyFans creator in the US who estimates that 60 to 70 percent of her content depicts some form of role-play, including step-“fauxcest,” is also concerned about the legislation. She says that the content she makes is “just playing a fantasy,” and that more sex education and literacy is required to teach people about the risks of various behaviors.

“I don’t think banning it is going to do anything,” she says. “If anything, it’ll just push even worse content that is actually illegal or immoral underground, so you’ll have to access it away from these platforms. This is just a slippery slope we’re getting into.”

The new UK law is seemingly targeted at the trend of choking in porn, which has been linked to real-life instances of fatal choking during sex. Though the practice is highly risky, potentially leading to brain damage or death, it’s increasingly common among young adults, largely due to the prevalence of eroticized choking depictions in the media and in porn. The death of a 16-year-old Australian girl in August, who authorities say lost consciousness after she was choked consensually during sex, has led to renewed calls for legislation banning depictions of the practice in online porn, which has “absolutely sort of normalized this kind of sexual violence,” Bertin recently told the BBC.

The UK has a history of imposing stringent restrictions on internet pornography, particularly compared to the United States, where statutes like Section 230 of the Communications Decency Act of 1996 largely exempt platforms from liability for hosting objectionable content. Long before many US states started requiring users to verify their ages before visiting porn sites, for instance, the UK communications regulation agency Ofcom has been slapping websites with fines of up to 10 percent of their worldwide revenue for failing to comply with age-verification requirements.

“The UK is really pushing for limits on free speech,” says Michael Fattorosi, an adult industry attorney based in Italy. “This is just something they're throwing on top of the heap of restrictions.”

In a statement to WIRED, Clara Coleman, a spokesperson for the UK’s Ministry of Justice, denied that the legislation was intended to curb sexual expression. “We’re committed to halving violence against women and girls in a decade, and that includes preventing harm before it starts,” Coleman says. “[These] laws aren’t about policing people’s sexual freedom, they’re about cracking down on content that normalizes violence against women, encourages unlawful behavior, and can cause real-world harm.”

Jackman, the lawyer, says that he has no issue with the provision banning strangulation, as its risks are proven and there are existing laws banning strangulation in the UK. He cautions, however, that the law is written broadly enough that relatively innocuous content could also potentially be penalized.

“It would be up to a court to decide this on a case-by-case basis, but given that it would appear that face-sitting by a vagina owner is now arguably suffocation, the insertion of a penis could equally be considered suffocation because it blocks the airways,” he says. In other words, “the UK could have just banned blow jobs” in online pornography.

Though the law currently applies only to consumers and creators in the United Kingdom, the stiff penalties for “possession” of such images—which the law defines as an image being stored on a phone, a hard drive, or in cloud storage—is of particular concern to Fattorosi.

“As far as creators are concerned, if you visit the UK, if you’re going there on holiday, if you’re going there for a conference, you should probably be careful about what you keep on your phone,” he says.

The subscription-based platform OnlyFans is particularly vulnerable, he adds. Though most American porn companies don’t have a substantial presence in the UK, making it difficult for Ofcom to go after them, that doesn’t apply to OnlyFans. The subscription-based platform’s parent company, Fenix International Limited, is based in the United Kingdom and has already faced steep fines from the agency for failure to adhere to age-disclosure requirements.

“If I were OnlyFans,” Fattorosi says, “I’d be very worried about this.” (In a statement, OnlyFans tells WIRED the platform “complies with applicable laws and regulations in all jurisdictions in which it operates and has longstanding, proactive safety measures in place that meet UK legal requirements.”)

In advance of the new legislation taking effect, adult content platforms have issued updated guidelines for creators. The platform IWantClips has already started complying, publishing a new set of guidelines for creators and fans based in the UK and removing clips that could potentially be swept up in the legislation. The laundry list of sexual niches and categories banned now includes “breast smothering,” “ass smothering,” “jeans facesitting,” “BBW facesitting,” “plastic fetish,” “scissorhold,” and “butt drops.”

Trans Princess Jezebel, a dominatrix and adult content creator in the UK, says she has already started going through her content library on IWantClips and OnlyFans, removing videos that may be seen as violative. She’s heavily reliant on clips to advertise her services as a dominatrix, and she’s concerned that once the law takes effect on November 6, she’ll take a huge financial hit.

“When the state starts regulating which types of pornography people are allowed to watch, a dangerous precedent is set,” she tells WIRED in an email. “Where will it end?”

But even though the UK is, for the time being, an outlier, Jackman warns it may not be for long. He says he has heard of lawmakers in Italy, the Netherlands, and Spain discussing similar legislation, as well as members of the evangelical lobby in the United States.

“In terms of free speech,” he says, “the lights are going out across the globe.”

SC Ventures and SBI Join Soter Insure's Series B Alongside Follow-On from Token Bay Capital

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The strategic investment from two global institutional-grade players, alongside follow-on support from Token Bay Capital, joins a Galaxy-led round that also includes Coinbase Ventures, Franklin Templeton and Brevan Howard Digital.

ABU DHABI, UAE, Nov. 5, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Soter Insure, a specialist insurer for the digital asset economy, today announced that SC Ventures, the innovation and ventures arm of Standard Chartered, and Japanese financial group SBI Group have joined its Series B as strategic investors, adding to the round led by Galaxy.

This second close also includes follow-on investment from Token Bay Capital, a Soter backer since its Series A. The new investors join Coinbase Ventures, Franklin Templeton and Brevan Howard Digital, which participated in the first close announced in July 2026.

SC Ventures and SBI join Soter Insure's Galaxy-led Series B, alongside follow-on from Token Bay Capital

Insurance capacity remains one of the main missing pieces for banks and asset managers entering digital assets. Without it, institutions cannot apply the same risk standards they use for every other asset class. The backing of two global financial groups that are active in digital assets reflects a growing recognition that specialized coverage is a prerequisite for institutional participation.

Soter Insure will use the proceeds to increase its insurance capacity, deepen its underwriting and technology capabilities, and expand into mature and emerging digital asset markets, including Asia-Pacific through its Hong Kong presence, Europe and the US.

"Banks can't scale in digital assets without the risk protection they rely on everywhere else, and today that protection is still scarce," said Henson Orser, Founder and CEO of Soter Insure. "With SC Ventures and SBI Group joining Galaxy, Coinbase Ventures and Franklin Templeton, we now have traditional finance and digital asset natives behind the same goal: insurance capacity at institutional scale. Our strategic investors now span most of the jurisdictions and networks at the forefront of digital asset adoption. We're grateful to Token Bay Capital for backing us again, and to Galaxy for leading the round."

Herve Francois, Principal, Digital Assets Fund, SC Ventures, said: "Insurance is a critical but often overlooked part of the digital assets ecosystem. Our investment reflects our belief that Soter is uniquely positioned to build a regulated insurance platform that can scale to meet institutional needs and support the industry's continued development globally."

Yangte Kim, Representative in the UAE of SBI Group, added: "As digital assets continue to gain broader adoption, we believe the development of robust insurance solutions will be an increasingly important part of the ecosystem. Soter brings together deep expertise across regulation, digital assets and traditional insurance to address this important market need. Through our investment, SBI Group looks forward to supporting Soter's growth and contributing to the continued development of a more resilient global digital asset ecosystem."

For more information about Soter Insure, visit soter.insure.

Headquartered in Abu Dhabi and operating out of Bermuda, with offices in London, New York and Dubai, Soter Insure was incubated by Further Ventures and WebN Group. The company offers specialized digital asset insurance products for financial institutions, including Directors & Officers (D&O), Professional Indemnity (PI), Crime, Specie, Slashing and Smart Contract Failure coverage, uniquely denominated in both fiat and native digital assets.

SC Ventures is Standard Chartered's innovation, fintech investment and venture-building platform. Since 2018, it has built, operated and scaled ventures, and made minority investments, deploying more than $1 billion across 30+ portfolio companies globally. It builds, invests in and partners with ventures shaping the future of financial services across digital banking, digital assets, SME growth, financial inclusion, trade and cross-border infrastructure. In the Middle East, SC Ventures has grown from 2 ventures to 10 in two years across the UAE and Saudi Arabia, with Egypt next on the roadmap, supporting regional growth through disciplined venture building, strategic partnerships and regulated innovation aligned with national economic visions.

Founded in 1999, the SBI Group is a comprehensive internet financial group and a pioneer of internet-based financial services in Japan. The Group operates a Financial Services Business centered on securities, banking, and insurance, providing highly convenient products and services at competitive prices through the internet. In addition, the SBI Group operates Asset Management Business, PE Investment Business, Crypto-asset Business, and Next Gen Business on a global scale.

Token Bay Capital is a venture capital firm investing in digital asset and blockchain infrastructure. Founded in Hong Kong in 2021 and now headquartered in Abu Dhabi Global Market (ADGM), Token Bay Capital Limited is authorized and regulated by the Financial Services Regulatory Authority (FSRA) as a Venture Capital Fund Manager. It backs early-stage companies building the infrastructure institutions need to use digital assets: custody, trading, payments, on-chain finance and tokenization.

Global Study: Canada Ranks Fifth Among 56 Commonwealth Countries for Youth AI Readiness, Yet Research from Ayre Think Tank Points to Gaps in Practical AI Use

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ayre Think Tank identifies potential C$100.85-billion AI-related GDP boost over the next decade though lower-scoring education and training provision could affect economic gains

ST JOHN'S, Antigua and Barbuda, Nov. 5, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Canada's strong connectivity and digital capabilities have placed it among the Commonwealth's leaders in youth AI readiness, but education and training provision emerges as a relative weakness, according to a comprehensive new international study by the Ayre Think Tank.

The inaugural Ayre Index ranked Canada fifth among 56 Commonwealth countries, behind Singapore, Malta, Australia and the United Kingdom, with an overall score of 70.2 out of 100. Canada scored 9.4 out of 10 for both meaningful access and digital, data, media and AI capability, compared with 5.3 for education and training delivery.

The findings were just released during the Commonwealth Heads of Government Meeting (CHOGM) in Antigua and Barbuda, attended by His Majesty King Charles III and Prime Minister Mark Carney.

Speaking about the Index, Calvin Ayre, Antiguan-Canadian philanthropist, entrepreneur and founder of the Ayre Think Tank, said: "I've spent my life in businesses where new technology changes everything, and I know you can't stand still. AI is going to change how we work, how we do business and how wealth is created. What matters is giving young people the skills and the chance to get ahead â whether to start businesses, find jobs or build something for themselves."

Ayre stressed that nobody can afford to be complacent right now. "Some Commonwealth countries are ahead, others have catching up to do. Nobody can afford to be complacent. That's why we created this Index. It shows countries where they stand, what they're getting right and where they need to do better. Governments now have to decide what they're going to do about it."

One key question facing Canada now is how it can convert its strong infrastructure and youth interest into practical capability-building among youth. The potential economic stakes are significant. Researchers applied International Monetary Fund (IMF) scenarios to Canada's economy and calculated that it could gain a potential AI-related GDP increase of C$100.85 billion over the next ten years under a moderate scenario. A conservative estimate is C$51.19 billion, and the optimistic case is C$150.44 billion.

"AI readiness is the minimum standard for future success. Canada needs to ensure its education systems equip young people to move beyond readiness and become AI fluent. While youth may be regular users of AI tools, the real priority is ensuring they know how to use AI effectively, question its outputs and harness its potential to build career skills and create opportunities," said Lisa Taylor, global authority on workforce systems and founder and CEO, Challenge Factory.

Developing AI fluency requires opportunities for informed, purposeful practice, yet, according to the Ayre Think Tank's analysis of OECD PISA 2025 data, only 37.7 per cent of Canadian 15-year-olds reported using AI chatbots for schoolwork at least weekly, compared with an OECD average of approximately 46 per cent. Separately, Statistics Canada's research found that just 10 per cent of employed 15- to 24-year-olds used generative AI at work over a 12-month period.

Moreover, the Index did not identify an explicit reference to AI in the teacher-policy evidence it used for Canada, drawing on UNESCO's country profile on technology in education. Together, these findings underscore the importance of strengthening AI education and practical skills development to better prepare young Canadians for an increasingly AI-driven workforce.

The Ayre Think Tank produced a white paper reporting on the findings from the Index, and the paper identifies five global priorities: affordable and reliable access to technology; education that develops practical skills; stronger routes from training into employment and enterprise; AI tools suited to local languages and needs; and safeguards that enable young people to question automated decisions.

It calls on governments, educators and development partners around the world to better understand the barriers young people face in each country before deciding where investment can make the greatest impact.

"We need to make sure that the next generation will be able to do more than just consume AI products made elsewhere; access must come with its own agency and a real equitable stake in how these systems are governed," said C. Justin Robinson, pro vice-chancellor and campus principal of the University of the West Indies, Five Islands Campus, who contributed to the white paper's foreword.

"Young people should be equipped to question new AI tools, to improve them and to be supported in building alternatives suited to their own needs â whether that happens, however, will depend on choices made now."

More about the Ayre Index and the Ayre Think Tank can be found at https://calvinayre.com/for-progress-think-tank.

BIGBANG Adds World Tour Finale at L.A.’s SoFi Stadium

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The K-pop veterans will complete their 20th anniversary tour with a one-off show at SoFi Stadium next March.

BIGBANG will bring their world tour to a close with a newly-announced concert next March in Los Angeles.

The K-pop veterans add a one-off show at SoFi Stadium on Saturday, March 13, 2027, for what will be the final date on their extensive 20th anniversary run.

Since getting underway in August, BIGBANG 2026-2027 World Tour ‘XX : COSMOS’ has visited stadiums across Asia, North America, Europe, the United Kingdom and Australia, and is now winding its way through another pan-Asian route. Next up, the first of two nights at Rajamangala National Stadium in Bangkok, Thailand this Saturday, Nov. 7. Explore Bigbang See latest videos, charts and news

This tour sees G-Dragon, Taeyang and Daesung reunite for the first time since 2017, and for the first time as a three-piece. When the group played MetLife Stadium in September, the setlist ran through 30 songs across about three hours, Billboard’s Jeff Benjamin reported, and included “BiiiG,” BIGBANG’s first new single in four years. “BiiiG” dropped on Aug. 19 to mark 20 years to the day since the group’s debut. Related Hitsujibungaku, Kyary Pamyu Pamyu & Otoboke Beaver to Play Japanese Music Showcase matsuri '26 in London M!LK Earns Second Japan Hot 100 No. 1 With 'That's! Chojo' as Vaundy's 'Shiwaawase' Reaches Top 3 Tinashe Sends Message to Republican Fans During 2026 Midterm Elections: 'Y'all Can Go'

Launched by YG Entertainment, BIGBANG arrived in August 2006 and broke new ground for the Korean-pop scene by winning the inaugural best worldwide act trophy onstage at the 2011 MTV Europe Music Awards. The following year, they became the first K-pop group to send an album into the Billboard 200 chart, when 2012’s ALIVE reached No. 150, and strut their live stuff with two world tours (hitting multiple U.S. arenas in 2012 and 2015).

In those 20 years, BIGBANG have landed five No. 1s on Billboard‘s U.S.-based World Digital Song Sales chart, and bagged their first Top 10 hit on the Billboard Global 200 with the special 2022 reunion single “Still Life” that marked T.O.P’s final release with the group.

The general onsale for the SoFi Stadium show opens Friday, Nov. 13 at 10am PT via Ticketmaster.

What the Democratic midterms sweep changes for U.S.-China relations

📅 2026-11-05  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

BEIJING — Democrats taking power from Republicans in Congress raises risks for U.S.-China tensions around Taiwan, while hardening tech competition, analysts said.

The Democrats swept this week's midterm elections, taking majorities in the House and Senate and flipping governors' offices in places like Texas, Nevada and Iowa. Most of the new election winners, however, won't begin their terms until January.

Before then, U.S. President Donald Trump and Chinese President Xi Jinping will likely maintain their fragile truce by meeting twice: in Shenzhen this month, and in Miami, Florida, next month.

"As a lame duck who is increasingly focused on his personal legacy rather than the Republican Party's success, Trump may resist increased pressure for confrontation with China and continue his pursuit of 'strategic stability,'" Gabriel Wildau, managing director at Teneo, said in an email.

High on the Democrats' agenda is removing Trump's tariffs, as part of a broader focus on addressing domestic inflation, CNBC has learned.

"On balance as a result of the midterms the Democrats may nudge the U.S.'s China policy in a more hawkish direction through hearings and legislation, particularly on economic security issues and Taiwan," said Scott Kennedy, senior advisor and trustee chair in Chinese business and economics at the Center for Strategic and International Studies.

But any efforts will "be constrained by the executive branch's wide range of latitude and China's growing power and leverage, which can't be easily undone," he said.

Beijing has hosted a flurry of global leaders this year, while major economies have struggled to slow a surge of imports from China. Chinese dominance in rare earths has given Beijing leverage in trade talks with the U.S. and the EU.

"Taiwan remains the most dangerous issue in U.S.-China relations and, in my view, the area where a Democratic Congress is most likely to challenge Beijing," wrote George Chen, partner and co-chair of digital practice at consultancy The Asia Group.

"Congress has repeatedly shown a willingness to pursue Taiwan initiatives even when administrations are more cautious," he said, noting Trump's balancing act will likely become more difficult.

Nancy Pelosi's visit to Taiwan while House Speaker in 2022 angered Beijing, which considers the democratically self-governed island part of its territory. The U.S. maintains unofficial relations with Taiwan under a "One China" policy.

Minority Leader Hakeem Jeffries (D-N.Y.), who is poised to become the first Black Speaker of the House in U.S. history, met in 2023 in New York with Tsai Ing-wen, who was then president of Taiwan.

During Trump's trip to Beijing in May, Xi warned that mishandling Taiwan would put U.S.-China relations in "great jeopardy."

Maintaining stability over Taiwan has grown more critical in the artificial intelligence race as the island is home to semiconductor giant TSMC, which manufactures many of Nvidia's chips.

Over the last several years, the U.S. government has tightened restrictions on the ability of Chinese companies to buy advanced chips from Nvidia, spurring Beijing's efforts to develop its own version of the technology.

U.S. stocks surged Wednesday after Trump accepted the Democrats' election wins, and oil prices fell following indications Washington would do more to end the Iran war. Apple and most "Magnificent Seven" tech stocks surged, despite declines in Nvidia.

But there's an "emergence of a broader consensus in Washington that America's technological lead should not be used to strengthen China's military or strategic capabilities," Chen said, noting a Democratic Congress may be motivated to be more aggressive.

"As a result, Beijing may discover that technology becomes a growing source of friction even if Trump and Xi continue to maintain relatively cordial summit diplomacy," he said.

The tech outlook ultimately depends on bigger questions about AI.

Chinese tech stocks climbed Thursday, extending a recent rebound from a sharp sell-off last month.

Beijing's latest regulatory intervention has helped support the market, said WisdomTree's Liqian Ren. But she said Chinese tech stock performance is ultimately tied more to global investor sentiment on AI.

"There's a lot of uncertainty on how much AI can deliver for U.S., or for China," she said.

—CNBC's Justin Papp and Kevin Breuninger contributed to this report.

BitMart founder confirms second exchange hack – Links to Mercado Bitcoin emerge

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Two exchange security incidents and a potential shared threat actor.

November has started with volatility. Macro FUD is driving uncertainty in the market. However, crypto security is also playing its part.

Binance’s exploit of a $6.3 billion hack triggered fresh panic, and now Mercado Bitcoin’s $38 million-$49 million exploit has added another layer of FUD over the past 24 hours.

The key takeaway? Another large-scale hack has occurred, which was confirmed by BitMart founder Sheldon Xia via his recent post on X. Initial investigation shows there may be a link between this and the attackers who exploited the $38M-$49M exploit on Mercado Bitcoin.

In essence, two potentially connected hacks have emerged within just 24 hours.

On-chain investigators are currently tracking down where the funds went. Interestingly on-chain data shows that around $11.45 million worth of altcoins has been moved between various wallets, raising concerns about potential liquidation.

This includes around $1.1 million worth of VIRTUAL being sent to a new wallet, with sales potentially taking place in batches of around $20,000. The attacker still holds around $1 million of VIRTUAL.

Timing is also important here. There is already a lot of crypto market FUD ahead of the US midterm elections. The Altcoin Season Index has also fallen below its third lower low, which means things are not looking great for altcoins.

With exchange hacks adding to the uncertainty, could rising sell pressure trigger a broader altcoin crash?

Altcoins face another test as the Mercado Bitcoin and BitMart hacks trigger fresh market concern.

The MercadoBitcoin hacker has moved to sell multiple altcoins such as PEPE, AAVE, UNI, ONDO, and ENA, converting them into ETH. The attacker now holds 7,988 ETH worth around $18.19 million.

Meanwhile, Arkham Intelligence reports that the hacker has moved 31 different altcoins to wallet 0x848 and sold them for around $316,000 in ETH. In essence, the hacker is rotating out of altcoins and moving into Ethereum.

As the largest altcoin by market cap, Ethereum is now in focus. From a technical perspective, Ethereum [ETH] is trading above $2.3k, and RSI remains in neutral territory. On the ETF side, however, flows have been weak, with Spot ETH ETFs recording seven consecutive days of net outflows.

This indicates that recent ETH strength has less to do with strong demand for ETH and more to do with Bitcoin weakness, with ETH dominance (ETH.D) up nearly 2% this week.

However, the bigger risk is what happens next.

If the hacker starts dumping his $18.19 million worth of ETH, Ethereum could get hit by negative selling pressure and push the sentiment lower.

With the altcoins already struggling, consecutive lower lows, weak ETF flows, and rising macro uncertainty, the market could face another liquidity squeeze, increasing the risk of a deeper altcoin crash.

Two linked exchange hacks have raised fresh fears, with hackers selling altcoins and moving millions into ETH.

Weak market sentiment, ETF outflows, and rising uncertainty could trigger more selling and a wider altcoin crash.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/regulatory/cms-finalizes-final-medicare-rule-deeper-cuts-physician-pay-delays-rpm-restrictions-2029" hreflang="en">CMS finalizes steeper Medicare physician pay cuts for 2027, delays restriction on third-party RPM vendors</a>

📅 2026-11-04  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
CMS finalizes steeper Medicare physician pay cuts for 2027, delays restriction on third-party RPM vendors" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

The Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services finalized sweeping Medicare payment reforms it says will shift the program from volume-based "sick care" toward prevention, primary care and accountable care.

The 2027 Medicare Physician Fee Schedule rule includes a roughly 2% reduction in baseline physician payment rates with targeted increases and new payment opportunities for accountable care, primary and chronic care and behavioral health. CMS finalized steeper physician payment cuts than it had proposed in July.

An unpublished PDF version of the 2,468-page final rule can be found here.

In a press release, CMS said it is "prioritizing prevention, strengthening primary care, cracking down on duplicate billing and reinforcing fiscal stewardship across Medicare."

The final rule modernizes physician payment structures to "reward health maintenance and coordinated primary care rather than disjointed, volume-based intervention," CMS said.

"Medicare should reward clinicians for keeping people healthy," HHS Secretary Robert F. Kennedy, Jr. said in a statement. "Under President Trump’s leadership, we are putting prevention and primary care at the center of Medicare, closing payment loopholes, and cracking down on waste. This rule directs resources toward coordinated care that puts patients first. We are changing what Medicare pays for to change what our health system delivers."

Under the final rule, CMS will officially sunset the traditional Merit-based Incentive Payment System (MIPS) reporting option beginning with the CY 2031 performance period. Clinicians will have through CY 2030 to transition to the MIPS Value Pathways (MVPs), which already cover approximately 98% of clinicians, or Advanced Alternative Payment Models. This reflects two additional years of transition time compared to the proposed rule. The rule also finalizes three new MVPs focused on diabetes, hypertension, and hospitalists, and requires clinicians to report at least one specialty-specific "Core Measure" starting in CY 2027.

For physicians, while the proposed rule includes statutory payment increases, Medicare payment rates face an overall baseline decrease for 2027 due to the expiration of a temporary 2.5% legislative increase from the One Big Beautiful Bill Act (OBBBA) in 2026.

Under the Medicare Access and CHIP Reauthorization Act (MACRA), the CY2027 conversion factors reflect statutory updates of +0.75% for qualifying Alternative Payment Model (APM) participants and +0.25% for non-qualifying participants. The changes to the PFS conversion factors for CY 2027 include these updates as required by statute and an estimated -0.12% adjustment necessary to account for proposed changes in work RVUs for some services, according to CMS in a fact sheet.

For clinicians who don't qualify through an advanced alternative payment model, the conversion factor drops from $33.40 to $32.63, a 2.31% reduction. For qualifying APM participants, the conversion factor drops 1.83% from $33.57 to $32.95. In the draft rule issued in July, CMS projected cuts of 1.68% and 1.19%, respectively.

CMS is also addressing what it sees as overpayment and duplicate reimbursement when multiple services are delivered during the same patient encounter. Under the final rule, when an office visit and minor procedure occur on the same day, CMS will reduce payment by 20% for the lower-cost service, reducing duplication in payment by patients and the Medicare program for clinical work already covered in the procedure’s valuation.

CMS had initially proposed paying the higher-cost service in full and reducing payment for the other service by 50%.

Physician groups were quick to criticize CMS' payment changes in the final rule.

"Notwithstanding modest policy improvements to enhance value-based care through MSSP, MGMA is disappointed by CMS’s decision to finalize several destabilizing policies in the 2027 Medicare Physician Fee Schedule," Anders Gilberg, senior vice president of government affairs at the Medical Group Management Association, said in a statement.

"Conversion factor cuts of 1.83% for qualifying APM participants and 2.31% for all other clinicians further degrade an already fragile payment system and amplify severe financial challenges caused by years of inadequate Medicare reimbursement," Gilberg said.

Compounding this ongoing reimbursement decline, CMS will cut payments by 20% when office visits billed with modifier 25 are performed on the same day as certain procedures, "interfering with physician practices’ ability to offer same-day care," he noted.

MGMA is urging Congress to intervene by immediately fixing cuts to the conversion factor, along with changes to modifier 25. "To prevent annual disruption, Congress should prioritize comprehensive MACRA reform by passing the Patients First Act," Gilberg said.

The agency is overhauling the office/outpatient evaluation and management visit complexity add-on code, HCPCS code G2211. CMS is transitioning HCPCS code G2211 from a flat add-on payment to a modifier that would increase the payment of the associated E/M code by 16%. That would maintain an equal percentage increase across all levels of E/M codes, CMS said.

The agency also finalized a new modifier that would offset the costs incurred by practitioners in accountable care organizations (ACOs) when providing longitudinal care and care coordination. The modifier would only be available for practitioners participating in a Shared Savings Program ACO or participant providers in a Long-term Enhanced ACO Design (LEAD) Model ACO and would increase payment of the associated E/M visit by 32%, CMS said.

The final 2027 PFS rule also establishes new 340B reporting requirements. Covered entities will have to report certain Medicare Part D claims involving drugs purchased through the 340B Drug Pricing Program to the Medicare Part D Claims Data 340B Repository beginning with claims with a date of service on or after Jan.1, 2027. The deadline for the first quarterly submission is delayed until Sept. 30, 2027.

In the final rule, CMS tightened remote patient monitoring and remote therapeutic monitoring requirements but backed off an immediate restriction on contractors.

Beginning in 2027, as CMS outlined in the proposed rule, RTM services can only be furnished to established patients, and providers must provide a separately reportable initiating visit before billing for RPM or RTM services. CMS initially proposed only allowing payment for RPM or RTM services when performed by clinical staff employed by the practice and not when those services are delivered by third-party vendors. The agency provided a two-year reprieve on that requirement as it goes into effect Jan. 1, 2029, "giving practices that rely on contractors two additional years to adapt," CMS said.

Health tech companies, providers and lawmakers pushed back on the proposal to restrict third-party vendors, urging CMS to reconsider the proposed changes to preserve patients' access to these tech-enabled services. Many rural, small, and independent practices, as well as both urban and rural health systems, rely on specialized clinical and technology partners to make remote monitoring services available to their patients.

CMS finalized changes to the Medicare Shared Savings Program, the nation’s largest value-based payment program, to "accelerate accountable care service delivery, further CMS’ goal of aligning spending and value in Original Medicare, and support achieving other related strategic objectives," the agency said in a fact sheet.

The overhaul to the MSSP program aims to make ACO participation more financially attractive, encourage organizations to take on risk, expand beneficiary participation and ease some quality-reporting requirements.

The agency says the changes are projected to grow the program by adding up to 1 million assigned beneficiaries in an average year and produce $5.5 billion in net federal savings over 10 years. CMS also projects the changes will generate an overall increase in shared savings net of losses to ACOs by $1.1 billion over a decade.

The final rule changes the Shared Savings Program’s benchmarking and financial methodology to balance incentives between Level E of the BASIC track and the ENHANCED track. The shared savings rate for Level E of the BASIC track increases 50% to 60% and reduce the maximum weight used in calculating the positive regional adjustment for lower-spending ACOs participating under the ENHANCED track from 50% to 35%.

The agency is increasing the prior savings adjustment's scaling factor from 50% to 75%, allowing more of an ACO's previous savings to be reflected in future benchmarks.

Under the final rule, CMS will risk-adjust the 5% cap on benchmark increases, potentially allowing ACOs that care for sicker, higher-cost patients to receive larger upward adjustments.

Data shows that patients aligned with an ACO are less likely to experience uncontrolled diabetes or hypertension and are more likely to receive recommended preventive care, according to CMS. In performance year 2024, 75% of the 476 participating ACOs earned shared savings totaling $4.1 billion, while generating net savings for the Medicare Trust Funds of approximately $2.5 billion compared to projected spending benchmarks.

Under the final 2027 PFS rule, approved ACOs may reduce or eliminate Part B cost-sharing for many items and services for assigned beneficiaries, directly lowering out-of-pocket costs for essential care. That change goes into effect April 1, 2027.

The rule also increases shared savings rates for eligible ACOs, provides new incentives for first-time participants and sets more predictable spending targets. And, the rule simplifies electronic health record reporting requirements, streamlines patient notice mandates and removes outdated administrative hurdles.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/finance/amwell-boosts-2026-guidance-after-strong-q3-boasts-government-business" hreflang="en">Amwell bets AI boom will boost demand for its unified care platform</a>

📅 2026-11-04  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
Amwell bets AI boom will boost demand for its unified care platform" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

As health plans and employers contend with a growing array of virtual care and AI-enabled tools, Amwell is positioning its digital health platform as the infrastructure layer for AI-enabled care.

Executives at Amwell, formerly known as American Well, touted the company's platform strategy, government contracts and higher-value clinical services as the main drivers of future growth during the company's third-quarter earnings call Tuesday.

Rising healthcare costs are pushing health plans and employers to adopt tech-enabled care solutions, but they are looking for measurable engagement and outcomes from digital health vendors, not just access, according to Ido Schoenberg, M.D., Amwell chairperson and CEO.

The proliferation of point solutions, accelerated by AI, is creating more vendor sprawl and member confusion, Schoenberg told investors and analysts during the call.

There's also a lack of proof with many digital health tools, he noted. "It is hard to see which programs work and harder still to replace one without disrupting members. That sprawl is accelerating. AI has made it faster and cheaper to build a clinical program. Investors have backed hundreds of new digital health companies this year, many of them built on AI. Our customers feel pressure to offer these programs, but each one brings its own cost, its own integration, and its own front door. The opportunity is real. So is the complexity," he said.

More patients are turning to AI for health guidance, but those interactions don’t necessarily lead them to appropriate, covered care, creating the risk of poor clinical decisions and unnecessary costs for health plans, Schoenberg said.

"This is the problem Amwell is determined to solve for our customers. We help them improve clinical and financial outcomes. We do it through activation, getting more of their members into technology-enabled care in place of conventional care that often costs more. We give our customers one platform under their brand with one integration. It provides reliable, scalable rails to bring in a program, exchange data with it, and tests whether it works. The member's experience stays consistent, simple, and engaging. A common data structure proves the outcomes and improves performance over time," he said.

As AI adoption grows, a unified care platform becomes more valuable, Schoenberg asserted.

"We are not selling an AI feature. We are the trusted platform where AI-powered care runs with clinicians in the loop," he said during the Q3 call.

Amwell's customers represent more than 90 million covered lives, and the company also serves 9.6 million beneficiaries of the Military Health System.

Amwell's platform can help drive member engagement and utilization of digital health tools, executives said.

"Most of the health plans we talk to still see engagement in the single digits. We address that with always-on engagement, outreach to members through our customers' own channels within the rules they set. When a customer switches it fully on, members use the platform at more than three times the rate we see when it's off," Schoenberg said.

The platform runs on a common data structure, he noted. "It sends a member into a program with the right information. It collects outcomes back, and it reports value in the customer's own terms, whether that customer is a health plan or an employer that the plan serves. That is how a program proves its return on investment and how it gets better over time," he said.

In response to a question from an analyst, Schoenberg suggested pricing for the solution could evolve from fixed Software-as-a-Service (SaaS) fees toward outcomes-based arrangements in which Amwell shares in the savings it produces for health plans.

Amwell executives view that platform strategy as key to the company's growth.

In Q3, Amwell reported revenue that was slightly above Wall Street's consensus and a narrower adjusted loss than analysts expected. Amwell executives also reiterated confidence in reaching positive operating cash flow and breakeven to positive adjusted EBITDA in the fourth quarter.

Amwell brought in $49 million in Q3 revenue, according to financial results published Tuesday afternoon. Revenue was down approximately 12.9% year-over-year, but $1 million above the high end of management's guidance range, according to Chief Financial Officer Mark Hirschhorn.

Subscription revenue was $23.9 million, down approximately 22.5% year-over-year and 6.8% sequentially, Hirschhorn said. “The year-over-year decline continues to reflect the previously disclosed churn we've discussed on prior earnings calls,” he added.

Amwell Medical Group (AMG) visit revenue was $23.7 million, up approximately 11.7% year over year, while AMG paid visits totaled approximately 301,000 visits, essentially flat year over year, Hirschhorn said.

Virtual primary care visits were up 41% year over year, continuing that segment's strong growth trajectory. Total platform visits were approximately 760,000 visits, down approximately 30.5% year over year—though Hirschhorn noted the decline is consistent with previously discussed portfolio changes.

The telehealth platform reported a net loss of $11.7 million during the quarter, compared to a loss of $29.3 million for the same period a year ago, Hirschhorn said. Adjusted EBITDA loss narrowed to $3.6 million from a loss of $12.7 million a year earlier.

Amwell executives are bullish on revenue growth from its own clinical services, including virtual primary care and behavioral health.

Executives also highlighted the company's government business as it delivered solid third-quarter financial performance. Amwell announced in September that the Department of Veterans Affairs (VA) issued a letter of intent to deploy its platform enterprise-wide.

"I want to be precise about what this is," Schoenberg said. "The letter is not a binding contract. A definitive agreement still has to be negotiated and executed under federal procurement rules, and that work is underway."

Schoenberg noted the letter followed a "comprehensive evaluation."

"It cited scalable video consultations, interoperability, cybersecurity compliance and support for integrated care delivery," he said. "In plain terms, the capabilities the department evaluated are the ones we already run at scale for the military health system, and the department's own scale is considerable."

Amwell executives are optimistic the company will sign a contract with the VA "over the next couple of months," Hirschhorn said, with implementation beginning in 2027, but no definitive agreement exists yet.

Moreover, Schoenberg said the platform is continuing to negotiate a "direct, longer-term contract" with the Defense Health Agency (DHA).

The DHA, which oversees services for the Military Health System, renewed its contract with the telehealth platform in August 2025. In June, it called for sole contracts with Amwell and four other vendors.

DHA discontinued its payment for behavioral health and automated care services with Amwell last year. Schoenberg said the cessation was due to the Department of Defense’s cost-cutting measures—not the department’s satisfaction with the services.

Hirschhorn said that Amwell is raising its full-year revenue guidance to between $204 million to $206 million, up from $195 million to $205 million. The company now expects full-year adjusted EBITDA to be a loss in the range of $6.9 million to $7.9 million, a meaningful improvement from its prior range of a loss of $12 million to $16 million.

Amwell forecasts full-year AMG visits between 1.32 million to 1.37 million visits.

“In summary, during the third quarter, we exceeded our revenue guidance with stronger visit volumes, a stable subscription base, and a leaner cost structure that positions us to finish the year on a strong note,” Hirschhorn said.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/providers/chutes-ladders-2026-hires-departures-firings-retirements-ceo-executives-healthcare" hreflang="en">Chutes &amp; Ladders—UHC names individual MA leader; HATCo CEO steps down</a>

📅 2026-11-04  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
Chutes & Ladders—UHC names individual MA leader; HATCo CEO steps down" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Welcome to this week's Chutes & Ladders, our roundup of hirings, firings and retirings throughout the industry. Please submit the good news—or the bad—from your shop, and we will feature it here each week.

> UnitedHealthcare has named Suha Assi as CEO of individual Medicare Advantage, per a LinkedIn post.

Assi previously spent nearly a decade as executive director for Medicare and Retirement in Tennessee, and she previously held roles based in the state for Cigna. She wrote in the post that the "relationships with our teams, providers and communities have shaped how I think about this business and, most importantly, the people we serve."

"As I step into this national role, I’m excited about what’s ahead for Medicare Advantage," she wrote. "I look forward to working alongside teams and provider partners across the country to modernize how we work together, strengthen relationships, and find new ways to deliver for our members."

> Daryl Tol, president and CEO of General Catalyst's Health Assurance Transformation Company (HATCo) is stepping down from the role, the venture capital firm's CEO Hemant Taneja shared Tuesday.

Tol had been with the firm for about four and a half years in the wake of his time as president and CEO of AdventHealth's Central Florida Division. During that time he headed HATCo's partner and advisory network—helping grow it to nearly 40 providers, payers and other portfolio companies—and took the company's top spot partway through 2025. Of note, he helped HATCo close its acquisition of regional health system Summa Health and served as its interim CEO for much of the past year until the appointment of Jennifer Eslinger last month.

Tol is being succeeded by Reva Nohria, a founding member of the HATCo team who has been with General Catalyst since 2018. Taneja, in his announcement, said her work at both organizations has encompassed investment, company creation and strategic partnership, and that she will continue as an investor at the venture capital firm alongside her new appointment.

Tol will be supporting a smooth leadership transition and stay on in an advisory role, a representative of General Catalyst told Fierce Healthcare.

> Jennifer Shuford, M.D., is leaving her post as deputy director and chief medical officer of the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention on Nov. 12, just four months after joining, according to a Washington Post report that was confirmed by the agency on Tuesday.

The agency told press in a statement that Shuford was departing "to spend more time with family." Her appointment was announced by President Donald Trump in April, and she joined in June.

Shuford, an infectious disease specialist came to the federal stage from a role as commissioner of the Texas Department of State Health Services, and before that served as the state's chief epidemiologist. Notably, she had been credited for heading Texas' response to a measles outbreak last year. Her departure comes as the CDC clashes with state health departments and other public health leaders over its response to a growing national measles outbreak.

Satish Pillai, M.D., is slated to fill the role in the interim as the agency seeks a replacement, the CDC said.

> Johns Hopkins Health Sysem CEO Kevin Sowers is set to retire at the end of 2027.

Sowers has served as president and CEO of the Johns Hopkins system since 2018. He also previously spent more than 32 years as a leader within the Duke University Health System, including eight as CEO of Duke University Hospital.

The early announcement of his retirement plans gives Johns Hopkins significant time to conduct a search for a successor, with Theodore DeWeese, dean of the medical faculty and CEO of Johns Hopkins Medicine, leading the charge. Sowers will become a strategic adviser to Johns Hopkins to support the transition, the health system said.

"When I joined Johns Hopkins Medicine in 2018, I made a commitment to the organization and to myself to give 10 years of everything I had to this extraordinary place—to the talented, dedicated people who work here, and the people and communities we serve. And I intend to keep that commitment," Sowers said in a video message.

> Calley Means, a prominent senior advisory to Health and Human Services Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr. closely aligned with the Make America Healthy Again Movement, resigned from his role on Thursday.

The resignation was reported by the Wall Street Journal and confirmed by Means on social media.

Media reports from the past several months described Means' frustrations seeing MAHA policy priorities delayed or blunted by others in the administration, with the journal citing sources describing him as "disillusioned." Still, his time advising the secretary notched several wins for the movement including overhauled dietary guidelines, steps toward greater food ingredient transparency and loosened restrictions on psychedelics as potential medical treatments.

Means wrote in his confirmation post that he still holds "tremendous respect for President Trump and Bobby Kennedy" in their battles against "the entrenched, dark forces in D.C. that profit from Americans getting sick."

"More policy and cultural change has happened around these goals in the past two years than any time in history—and I know this movement is just getting started," he wrote.

> Premier Health, a five-hospital nonprofit academic system in Southwest Ohio, has appointed Rosanna Morris as its upcoming president and CEO, effective in the first quarter of 2027.

Morris is currently executive vice president and chief clinical operating officer at the University of Texas MD Anderson Cancer Center, and was previously president of Beaumont Hospital-Royal Oak. She is a registered nurse by training and has more than 27 years of health leadership.

She takes over for the retiring Michael Riordan, who has led Premier Health for about half a decade. During that period, he's credited, by the system's board, with stabilizing the system's finances.

> Centivo has added two leaders to its C-suite, the health plan announced.

Lisa Bielamowicz, M.D., will join the company as chief health officer, while James Parr will step into the role of chief commercial officer. Both are veteran healthcare leaders, Centivo said.

Bielamowicz has a long career as a physician and health system consultant, and will take on the chief health officer title after advising Centivo's board of directors.

Parr has previously served as a senior adviser to employers at Lockton as well as senior vice president and head of sales at Collective Health. Parr has also held leadership roles at Cigna, per the announcement.

"There are few leaders in healthcare who have the health benefits strategy knowledge and have built the strong relationships that Lisa and James bring to Centivo," said Ashok Subramanian, co-founder and CEO of Centivo, in the announcement. "These new leaders put Centivo in a strong position as we launch in new markets and help more employers across the country tackle rising healthcare costs and restore affordability by rethinking their healthcare benefits strategy."

Mansue will have been at the helm for six years, and is credited by the organization for expanding local care access and championing a culture of wellness among its 7,800-person workforce.

Moore has led Inspira's operations since 2021 and was promoted to president at the top of this year. He has 35 years of industry experience and will most immediately prioritize the opening of a new hospital tower and building upon the system's recent Epic launch.

“Leading Inspira has been one of the great privileges of my career,” Mansue said in the announcement. “I’m proud of what our team has accomplished and grateful for the trust of our communities. After working alongside Warren for many years, I know his ability to bring people together, navigate complex challenges and understand what our patients and neighbors count on us to deliver. I have every confidence in his leadership of Inspira’s next chapter.”

> Jonathan B. Perlin, M.D., Ph.D., will retire from his roles as president and CEO of accrediting organization Joint Commission on Dec. 31. Current Chief Operating Officer James I. Merlino, M.D., is slated to succeed him.

Perlin boasted three decades of healthcare executive leadership across his career, which included five at the top of Joint Commission. During that period he oversaw a substantial reduction on Joint Commission standards deemed to be burdensome or obsolete, and programs to support healthcare safety issues in low- and middle-income countries. Titles preceding his time at the organization included president of clinical operations and chief medical officer for HCA Healthcare, and the U.S. Department of Veteran's Affairs' under secretary for health.

Perlin will remain a strategic advisor to the group following his retirement, holding the title of president emeritus.

Merlino's career included leadership roles at Cleveland Clinic such as chief clinical transformation officer and chief experience officer, as well as at healthcare performance improvement company Press Ganey.

> Robert C. Garrett, CEO of Hackensack Meridian Health, will retire next year after decades with the organization, including its formative 2016 merger.

Garrett is set to step down on June 30, upon which time he'll hold the role of CEO emeritus for a year to help ease the transition. The 18-hospital, nearly $10 billion nonprofit said it plans to announce his successor before the year's end.

Garrett began his career in the early 1980s at Hackensack University Medical Center, and in 2009 was named president and CEO of Hackensack University Health Network. When the organization merged with Meridian Health in 2016 to form New Jersey's largest health system, he served as co-CEO for two years before taking on the solo mantle.

Alongside the organization's growth to more than 500 patient care locations, Garrett's tenure also marked the opening of the Hackensack Meridian School of Medicine, New Jersey's first new medical school in decades.

"Bob has been the driving force behind HMH's unprecedented growth and transformation, its superior quality, innovation and commitment to building an exceptional workforce," Board Chair Keith Banks said in a statement. "At the heart of Bob's legacy is his unwavering belief that we must deliver the most innovative and compassionate care to all patients. HMH is solidly positioned for continued growth, and we are profoundly grateful to Bob and extend our warmest wishes to him for this well-deserved next chapter."

> The American Medical Association tapped Jennifer Goldsack as senior vice president and inaugural leader of the AMA Center for Digital Health and AI. Goldsack joins the AMA from the Digital Medicine Society (DiMe), a global non-profit organization dedicated to advancing digital medicine to optimize health, where she was both founder and CEO.

A leading voice on the responsible adoption of digital health and AI in healthcare, Goldsack has a record of bringing together evidence, clinical practice, technology and policy to help ensure digital innovations are safe, effective, equitable and capable of delivering meaningful value for patients and physicians, according to the AMA.

"We are at a defining moment for medicine. Digital health and augmented intelligence are rapidly and fundamentally changing how and where care is delivered. For these tools to reach their full potential and be effective for patients, physicians must lead the way in developing and implementing them. Jennifer is the right leader for this moment because she knows how to bring physicians and innovators together and translate that collaboration into real-world impact on patient care," AMA CEO John Whyte, M.D., said.

“Reimagining how we care for people in the digital era has been the focus of my career, and we are at an extraordinary moment of opportunity,” Goldsack said in a statement. “We have powerful new tools at our disposal and a responsibility to make sure we harness them to improve people’s lives. I’m thrilled to join the AMA and work alongside physicians and partners across healthcare. We’re going to set a high bar for what better healthcare looks like in the digital and AI era. And together, we will make it happen.”

Since its launch last year, the AMA Center for Digital Health and AI has produced key research, including: a survey of AI usage among physicians, showing growing confidence in the technology’s clinical advantages; and an international survey on wearables.

Hilzinger.

>Centene announced that Kenneth A. Burdick has retired from the company's board of directors, effective July 28, and that Paul J. Diaz has been appointed to the board, effective immediately.

Burdick joined the Centene board of directors in January 2022, offering insight that drew upon his long-standing leadership in healthcare. He served as executive vice president of products and markets at Centene and before that was the chief executive officer of WellCare Health Plans.

Diaz is currently a managing partner at Cressey & Company, a private investment firm focused on cultivating high-quality healthcare firms. Before joining Cressey, he was president and CEO of Myriad Genetics Inc., as well as CEO of Kindred Healthcare. He has also served on the board of healthcare organizations, such as DaVita and PharMerica.

"Ken has been a valued member of our Board. His deep industry expertise, strategic perspective, and commitment to improving healthcare have made a meaningful impact on Centene. On behalf of the Board, we thank him for his service and wish him all the best in retirement," said Frederick H. Eppinger, Chair of Centene's Board of Directors. "We are also pleased to welcome Paul Diaz to the Board. His extensive healthcare leadership experience, public company governance expertise and record of guiding organizations through growth and transformation will bring valuable perspective as Centene continues to advance its strategy and serve its members."

Seabra joined Healthineers in 2001, and from 2015 to 2018, he led the Western Europe and West Africa regions. In his most recent role as head of enterprise services, he quadrupled its revenue over the last eight years.

“We would like to thank John for his many years of successful service and outstanding commitment to Siemens Healthineers, first within our Varian business and later as President and Head of our Americas business. We are very pleased to appoint João Seabra as our new President and Head of the Americas. Under his leadership and thanks to his innovative thinking, Siemens Healthineers paved the way in our industry by creating Value Partnerships of increasing scope and impact with key customers,” said Darleen Caron, Member of the Managing Board at Siemens Healthineers with responsibility for the Americas and Chief Human Resources Officer.

In his new role at PayZen, Wu will lead the company’s engineering organization, overseeing platform architecture, AI infrastructure and product engineering.

Before joining Availity, Alphs Jackson held roles in physician and payer leadership, where she integrated value-based care models with clinical operations. In her new role as CMO, she will guide the responsible usage of emerging technologies, as part of Availity’s clinical strategy.

>Vanderbilt Health has chosen David C. Miller as the successor to Jeff Balser as President and CEO and Dean of Vanderbilt University School of Medicine.

Miller is currently serving as Chief Executive Officer of Michigan Medicine and Executive Vice President for Medical Affairs for the University of Michigan and will assume the role at Vanderbilt on January 1, 2027. Earlie this year, Balser announced plans to retire on December 31, after serving Vanderbilt in leadership roles for nearly two decades.

Miller’s experience at the similarly sized Michigan Health uniquely prepares him to become Vanderbilt Health’s new leader, the health system said. Miller’s selection as the new senior leader for Vanderbilt Health and Vanderbilt University School of Medicine finalizes an extensive national search by the VH Board of Directors to identify Balser’s successor.

As Chief Executive Officer, Miller will be responsible for shaping the future strategic direction of Vanderbilt Health and maintaining the scholarly activities shared by the Medical Center and University.

“From an incredibly strong field of applicants, Dr. Miller’s personal warmth, prior leadership experiences, and commitment to organizational culture stood out. >Aledade appointed Darren Thayne as chief technology officer and hired Josh C. Mandel, M.D. as chief scientist. Thayne and Mandel will bring deep expertise in enterprise scale and open data standards to the company, Aledade said.

Leveraging their combined experience, Aledade will further integrate advanced AI into its platform.

Thayne joins Aledade from Domo, where he spent 15 years leading its technology team, as founding technology leader, chief technology officer and executive vice president of product. He was responsible for breakthrough technology and deployment of AI-based tools that were central to customer experience.

Dr. Mandel, who is both a physician and software engineer, joins Aledade from Microsoft research, where he helped build open standards such as FHIR (Fast Healthcare Interoperability Resources). His work will focus on external interoperability and data exchange efforts, as well as AI strategy.

“AI raises the stakes on that alignment. Deployed inside fee-for-service, it accelerates billing. Deployed inside our value-based care model, it accelerates health. Daren and Josh have built infrastructure and open standards at national scale, and they're joining us to make sure that the value created by AI in healthcare flows to patients and practices,” said Farzad Mostashari, M.D., co-founder and CEO of Aledade.

>Brian S. Kim, M.D. has been named the first vice president and chief biotechnology officer at Northwell Health, as well as head of intellectual asset management at the Feinstein Institutes. He will also join the department of dermatology as a professor at Northwell Health.

The Feinstein Institutes is home to Northwell Health’s research center, and it serves as the largest health care provider and private employer in New York State. Kim joins Feinstein from the Icahn School of Medicine at Mount Sinai, bringing his expertise in dermatology, medical research and biotechnology.

His work has led to significant clinical advances, including the design of pivotal clinical trials that resulted in two first-in-class U.S. Food and Drug Administration-approved treatments for atopic dermatitis and prurigo nodularis. He has founded three biotech companies, including Alys Pharmaceuticals, and authored over 190 peer-reviewed publications.

Kim will continue to advance Feinstein Institute’s groundbreaking research on electric stimulation to treat disease, including work on nerve stimulation implants that could potentially treat Crohn’s disease and other inflammatory conditions.

“Dr. Kim’s appointment reflects our commitment to transforming bold research into better outcomes for our patients,” said John D’Angelo, M.D., Northwell’s president and CEO. “With his leadership, we can better translate breakthrough science and pioneer the future of medicine while staying grounded in our mission to produce knowledge to cure disease.”

>The University of Oklahoma’s academic health system (OU Health) has named Jonathan W. Curtright as the new president and CEO, effective July 1, 2026. Richard P. Lofgren, M.D., OU Health’s inaugural president and CEO, announced his retirement earlier this year and Curtright will be his replacement.

Curtright has been chief operating officer of OU Health since August 22, and under his leadership, the system saw an annual revenue increase from $1.9 billion to a projected $3.7 billion in fiscal year 2026. Admissions also increased 22.3% percent and outpatient clinic visits statewide reached more than 1 million, reflecting higher patient volumes and service intensity as opposed to acquisitions.

He was also responsible for program expansions, including investments in pediatric health, cancer centers and neonatal intensive care.

OU Health board of directors Chair G. Rainey Williams Jr. said, “Jonathan has been instrumental in helping advance OU Health’s mission and strategic vision during a period of extraordinary growth. He understands the unique responsibilities of an academic health system and has demonstrated the leadership, operational expertise and long-term vision needed to guide OU Health into its next chapter. The board is confident in his ability to build upon the strong foundation established under Dr. Lofgren’s leadership.”

>Christopher O’Connor, the CEO of Yale New Haven Health System (YNHHS), will be leaving after 14 years with the organization.

He will serve as a special advisor to the chair of the Board of Trustees during the transition, and the board will also be conducting a CEO search over the next months. Pamela Sutton-Wallace, the current president of YNHHS, will serve as acting CEO, effective immediately.

YNHHS has been well positioned by O’Connor to open the Adams Neurosciences Center in 2027, which is the largest strategic project in the history of the academic health system, according to the release.

Wathen was initially named senior vice president and CFO of Deaconess in 2010, after serving as manager of internal audit, manager of finance, CFO of The Women's Hospital and controller of Deaconess Health System.
